- **Run 3 (05:35:51Z → 07:02:01Z, log `./tmp/logs/2026-10-17-cheri-acceptance-3.log`) — the probe COMPLETED ON BOTH LANES: riscv64 run exit 0 and Morello run exit 0 (the lane's FIRST full corpus run, with round 36's `--target` fix; deploy 142/143 units — the one failure is an LLVM **backend crash** in `cfi-call-mid-func`, `Should only get a blockaddress for the current function`, a genuine per-record toolchain limitation for the classification), each stamp carrying `guest-status.txt` (`run-test-exit: 0`) and a 246-record `results.json`.** It also exposed a SECOND-ORDER driver race — the prompt gate is necessary but NOT sufficient: lane 1's deploy passed (142 files, the same two predicted-class failures), but in the guest the driver re-sent cmd 6 (`cp`) when **cp's own completion prompt** appeared before the typed token echo surfaced, then re-sent cmd 7 (the runner) when the **duplicate cp's prompt** appeared before the queued runner started — **3 runner invocations in the transcript**. A prompt can also mean "an earlier queued command finished and the pending one has not started", and the typed line can carry line-editing control chars (`BENCH_D ^HIR`), so neither the prompt nor an exact-text echo match is a reliable discriminator. Since round 36 the driver sends only at a prompt with `--settle`, so the dropped-typeahead case is impossible on these lanes — **the re-send buys nothing and only duplicates**. **User decision 2026-10-17: run 3 finishes as a TIMING PROBE only (its stamp is NOT acceptance evidence); the next fix round removes the re-send entirely.** Evidence: **`./tmp/logs/2026-10-17-cheri-acceptance-run3-driver-reentrancy.txt`**.

On this image `kldstat -q -m virtio_p9fs` **did not see the already-loaded module**, so the `kldload` ran and failed — `kldload: can't load virtio_p9fs: module already loaded or in kernel` — and **a failing *last* member of an AND-OR list is fatal under `set -e`**, so the runner died inside `ensure_p9fs` and its EXIT trap wrote the initial `not-started`. Its own comment claimed the guard "keeps a re-run quiet" — it does not.   - **A correction to the planner's first read of this log, recorded rather than smoothed:** the driver **DID** send `PS1='SPIKEPROMPT> '` **and** its first command — both are in the file (`PS1='SPIKEPROMPT> '`, then `kldstat -q -m virtio_p9fs || echo "virtio_p9fs not auto-loaded"`) — sent **while the motd was still printing**, i.e. **before the shell's first prompt existed**. The planner's `tail -c 700` window hid them and a `grep -c` that returned **1** was misread as zero; the full-file dump at the cap settled it. (Same class as this session's recurring lesson: a window at the point of *injection* is not the point of *effect* — read the whole artifact.)
  - **Root cause, now precise — a self-match:** `console-driver.py`'s `if PROMPT in buf` is a **substring test, and the echoed input it just sent contains the marker** (`PS1='SPIKEPROMPT> '` *contains* `SPIKEPROMPT> `), so the state machine satisfied itself with its own echo and advanced early — the same defect class as the `pgrep -f` wrapper self-match above. On riscv64 that raced through harmlessly (the shell then applied `PS1` and printed the real marker); on Morello the shell never printed `SPIKEPROMPT> `, so `await-prompt` waited forever — **and that state has no timeout**, so the cap went by with only the first command sent and never executed (no `kldstat` output anywhere in the log).
  - **A scaffolding defect, not a plan or pin failure.** Round 30's brief carries the corrected fix: sync on a **bare-line token** (self-match-proof), give every wait a timeout with a fallback, and prefer the prompt the guest actually prints.

- **Round 26 (12:08–12:56, exit 0) verdict: `build_result: fail`, one blocking issue (`riscv64-sstc-menvcfg-panic`) — and it wrote BOTH its session report and the issue.** riscv64 got past firmware load — `bbl loader` → `---<<BOOT>>---` → FreeBSD 15.0-CURRENT → FDT parsed → full device probe → `Device configuration finished.` — then panicked at clock init: `Illegal instruction 0x14d51073 at 0xffffffc00070769a`, i.e. `csrw stimecmp, a0` in `riscv_timer_et_start` (CSR 0x14d; the panic's `DELAY+0x43c` attribution is mis-symbolization — verified by disassembling the pinned kernel). **Morello booted to `login:` on the CURRENT reassembled bytes** (380 s; `build/cheri/logs/spike-morello-boot2-netnone.log`), so that lane's argv and firmware are sound. Both image sha256s asserted unchanged before and after; no stray processes left; `scripts/cheri/guest/runner` written; the three candidate I/O tests **not** run and `versions-spike.txt` deliberately not created (a boot failure stops the step). The chain, all point-of-effect in the issue: the DT advertises Sstc (`smode_extensions`) → the kernel sets `has_sstc` (`identcpu.c:466`) → takes the `csr_write(stimecmp)` path (`timer.c:92-93`) → QEMU gates that CSR on `mcounteren.TM` **and** `menvcfg.STCE` (`csr.c:886-889` — the specification, not a quirk) → BBL writes `mcounteren` only (`minit.c:71`) and **never** `menvcfg` → illegal instruction.
- **User decision 2026-10-16: stop advertising Sstc — A/B-verified by the planner BEFORE briefing.** `QEMU_CPU_CHERI_RISCV64` becomes **`rv64,sstc=off`**. Same pinned artifacts, two boots (`tmp/verification/2026-10-16-cheri-lanes-bringup-diag-sstc.sh`): **control → panic** (the exact round-26 failure) / **`sstc=off` → `login:`** (killed by the 600 s cap at the prompt) / **disk sha256 byte-identical afterwards** (`afdcb05d…e9121`). The spelling is safe because it is the lane's **own** CPU model, not a swap: `virt`'s `default_cpu_type` is `TYPE_RISCV_CPU_BASE`, which for this build is the concrete `rv64` (`virt.c:1646`; `cpu.h:49,63-65`; `DEFINE_CPU` at `cpu.c:1632`) — one property changes, and **CHERI comes from the xcheri binary**, proven by a *purecap* kernel booting under it. The kernel then uses its own `sbi_set_timer` fallback, which this BBL does implement (`sources/bbl/machine/mtrap.c:92,171`) — so the advertisement and the firmware finally agree. **No source deviation, no rebuild, and no artifact pins move: Morello's round-26 boot proof stands, and round 27 re-proves riscv64 only.**

Recorded in `versions-image.txt` as `qemu-bios-cheri-riscv64`. The pinned `QEMU_BIOS_CHERI_RISCV64` names it.

- **⚠ The side effect, and why it is recorded rather than glossed:** installing the bbl writes into `output/sdk/`, which cheribuild reads as "the SDK changed", so it considered the worlds stale and **re-installed both** (install-only — 106.7 s / 144.1 s, not a recompile), repackaged both sysroots, and **reassembled both disk images**. Both sha256s therefore moved, and the pin-fill re-took them (verified: `env.sh` matches the artifacts byte-for-byte). The riscv64 `-kernel` file is untouched (same size, same mtime). **The new images were re-verified, not assumed**: both still carry `p9fs.ko`, `p9fs_mount`, `virtio_p9fs.ko` and `loader.efi` (so the p9fs withdrawal holds on the current bytes too). **This is the plan's own image/pin feedback loop firing for real** — a rebuild re-takes the pins, and anything proven against the old bytes must be re-proven.
- **Consequence for the spike:** the earlier "Morello's boot needs no re-proving" is **corrected** — its proof ran against the superseded image, so round 26 boots **both** lanes and records both logs.
- **Step 7 round 1 (round 24) verdict: `build_result: fail`, one blocking issue — `riscv64-missing-bbl-firmware`.** The `aarch64-morello` lane **booted end to end on the first attempt** — EDK2 `BdsDxe` → `loader.efi` Revision 3.0 → FreeBSD 15.0-CURRENT → `Starting sshd.` → `login:` (killed by the 480 s cap at the prompt; `build/cheri/logs/spike-morello-boot1-netnone.log`) — **so Morello's boot needs no re-proving.** The `cheri_riscv64` lane exited 1 in under a second with one line: `qemu-system-riscv64xcheri: Unable to load the RISC-V firmware "bbl-riscv64cheri-virt-fw_jump.bin"` (`build/cheri/logs/spike-riscv64-boot1-netnone.log`). Both pinned images' sha256 were asserted unchanged before and after the spike.
- **Root cause (planner-verified in-tree):** the CHERI riscv64 machine loads a **BBL firmware** (`sources/qemu/include/hw/riscv/boot.h:30` = `RISCV64_BIOS_BIN`) into M-mode and then jumps to the `-kernel` payload. The plan's boot-vehicle decision was right that `-kernel` is mandatory and **wrong that it is sufficient**. cheribuild's own riscv64 flow always emits `-bios <bbl>` (`run_qemu.py:245-262`) and lists `bbl-baremetal-riscv64-purecap` as a dependency (`run_qemu.py:938`) — a target this plan never invoked. `output/sdk/share/qemu/` carries `edk2-*` and `opensbi-*` but no `bbl-*`.

**Coder: STEP 10 PRE-FIX 3 — remove the console driver's sync-state re-send (round 37). One behavioural change in one file; the gate is the scripts verifier, now 106 checks and PROVEN RED at 3/106.**
- **The finding (point of effect, run 3's console):** the **prompt-gated** re-send fires falsely. `cp`'s **own completion prompt** appeared before the typed token echo surfaced → cmd 6 re-sent; the **duplicate cp's prompt** then preceded the queued runner → cmd 7 re-sent → **3 runner invocations**. A prompt can also mean "an earlier queued command finished and the pending one has not started", and typed lines can carry line-editing control chars (`BENCH_D ^HIR`), so an exact-text echo match is fragile. Since round 36 the driver sends only at a prompt with `--settle` quiescence, so the dropped-typeahead case cannot occur — **the re-send only duplicates**. Evidence: `./tmp/logs/2026-10-17-cheri-acceptance-run3-driver-reentrancy.txt`.
- **The fix (as your FIRST edits, `scripts/cheri/console-driver.py`):**
  1. In the `sync` state, **delete the re-send branch entirely** — no `send(cmd_lines[...])` and no re-`send("echo %s" % token)`. A missing token now means exactly one thing: wait to the deadline, then take the existing fallback (log + proceed, or break for the sentinel).
  2. **Remove the `--resend-timeout` argument** and every reference to it (module docstring, help, any constant) — dead config, and the plan's rule is that no knob may exist that no code path consumes. `run.sh` passes no override (verify by grep).

It then died 22 minutes in, building **Morello's world**: `Invalid SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` → `*** [vers.c] Error code 1` → `Fatal error (in target cheribsd-morello-purecap)`.
- **Root cause — our own `env.sh` export, killed by a GNU-vs-BSD `date` difference.** `stand/common/newvers.sh:47-51` validates the variable BSD-style, `date -ur $SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`; GNU `date -r` reads its argument as a **filename**, so on this host `date -ur 1791763200` → `No such file or directory` → the script always takes its failure branch. The value arrives by inheritance: `env.sh:56` pins it, **`env.sh:85` exports it**, and `build_image.sh:60` sources `env.sh`, so cheribuild and everything under it — including FreeBSD's bootstrap — see it. It is a reproducibility pin for the bench's own builds and was never meant for CheriBSD. **riscv64 escaped only because its world was built by the C23 diagnostic, which invoked cheribuild directly and never sourced `env.sh`**; attempt 5 reused that world in 43 s without re-running `newvers.sh`. Morello was the first **fresh** world build under this environment. **Proven at the point of effect**: re-running the exact failing command with the pin set reproduces `Invalid SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`/exit 1; with it empty, exit 0 and `vers.c` is written. Evidence: `./tmp/logs/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-host-image-attempt6-findings.txt`.
- **The change — ONE line, on the two CheriBSD invocations only.** In `scripts/cheri/build_image.sh`, on the two `run_cheribuild` lines for the CheriBSD lane targets (currently `:170` and `:172`), change
  `--cheribsd/extra-env="CPATH=$ROOT/$CHERI_CROSS_CPATH"`
  → `--cheribsd/extra-env="CPATH=$ROOT/$CHERI_CROSS_CPATH SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH="`
  and extend the comment block above them so a future reader does not "tidy away" the empty assignment: record the BSD/GNU `date -r` mismatch and that an **empty** value is what makes `newvers.sh` take its `else date` branch.
  - **Both entries must ride in ONE space-separated value — this is not cosmetic.** Verified with `--pretend`: a **second `--cheribsd/extra-env` flag OVERWRITES the first**, and the two-flag spelling silently dropped `CPATH` from the resolved list (`['SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=']` only) — which would have re-broken stage 1.1, the very failure the shim exists to prevent. The single space-separated value resolves correctly to `['CPATH=<shim>', 'SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=']`, and that pair is confirmed on **7 `make.py` command lines** of the pretend output (`env MAKEOBJDIRPREFIX=… CPATH=<shim> SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH= CC=…`). The empty value survives the whitespace split intact.
  - Nothing else changes: **do not touch `env.sh`** (the pin and its export stay — other lanes' consumers are unaffected by this leg-scoped neutralisation), and `build_emulator.sh` / `stage_sources.sh` are not involved.
- **The verifier** is planner-owned and gains two checks for this: **80 checks, red at 78/80**, failing on exactly those two (`neutralises SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH on the CheriBSD legs (set EMPTY)` — every CheriBSD invocation must carry it and must carry it empty; and `rides SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH in the SAME extra-env value as CPATH`). Both are **proven red** against the current tree. Re-run it and turn it green.

- **Root cause — a Plan Error, and the plan's own pin was the bug.** `disk_image.py` uses makefs to build the disk's **EFI FAT partition** (`-t msdos`). And since `Makefile.boot:41` uses `+=` **last**, no value supplied from outside — command line, environment, or `src-env.conf` — can ever win. **That whole family of fixes is dead, not just that spelling.** The planner verified the injection point but never the *effect*; that is the lesson, and for this revision the mechanism was proven end-to-end **before** the brief was written.

  2. **Add `scripts/cheri/glibc-c23-shim/{string.h,wchar.h,stdlib.h}`.** Each `#include_next`s glibc's own header and then undefines the C23 const-generic macro layer; the plain C17 declaration sits directly above each macro in glibc's header, so removing the macro restores the pre-C23 return type. The exact tested content is on disk at `build/cheri/scratch/glibc-c23-shim/` — **copy it verbatim**, adjusting only the leading SCOPE comment to say it is the shipped copy. The working content is:

     - `stdlib.h`: `#include_next <stdlib.h>` then `#undef bsearch`
     These lists are the **complete** const-generic symbol set for this glibc (11 symbols across 3 headers), enumerated from glibc's own headers — one missing symbol is another failed build, so the verifier asserts each list as a set.
  3. **`env.sh`** — one new pin in the CHERI block, a repo-relative path and **one space-free token**: `CHERI_CROSS_CPATH="scripts/cheri/glibc-c23-shim"`, with its name added to the same `export` line.
  4. **`scripts/cheri/build_image.sh`** — on the **two CheriBSD lane invocations only**, pass `--cheribsd/extra-env="CPATH=$ROOT/$CHERI_CROSS_CPATH"`. **Not** on the `makefs-linux` leg. Add the shim to the existing preflight block (die naming it if `$CHERI_CROSS_CPATH/string.h` is missing), mirroring the staged-tree preflights. The round-9 world-first structure stays **exactly** as it is.
- **Why this works — proven at the point of effect, not the point of injection.** The diagnostic (`./tmp/verification/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-step6-diag.sh`, log `./tmp/logs/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-host-diag-cpath.log`) ran the real `buildworld` with `--cheribsd/extra-env=CPATH=<shim>` and **carried stage 1.1 through to stage 4.2** — `>>> stage 1.1` → `1.2: bootstrap tools` → `2.3: build tools` → `3: cross tools` → `3.1` → `4.1: building includes` → `4.2: building libraries` — with `getopt.c`/`getopt_long.c` producing only `-Wcast-qual` warnings. That is past everything attempts 3 and 4 died at. A direct compile test also showed **zero** diagnostics, not merely an error demoted to a warning.
  - **Why CPATH is the right layer:** it is read by the *compiler*, so it needs no make-variable cooperation at all — it sidesteps the exact hop that failed.
  - **It is self-scoping.** CPATH is searched after every `-I`, and the FreeBSD cross-build stage resolves `<string.h>` from its own sysroot via `-I`, so the shim cannot leak into the world build — it applies only to the host-tools stage that needs it. `#undef` of a name that is not a macro is a no-op, so it is inert on any glibc predating this change.
  - **`CCC_OVERRIDE_OPTIONS` was tested and rejected** as the alternative: it works, but prints 2 lines on *every* clang invocation (tens of thousands across a world build) and treats the symptom instead of restoring the C23-era type.

**Superseded brief (makefs portability round — landed in round 8):**
- **The change:** the pinned 2021 makefs fork does not compile under this host's **GCC 15.2.0**, because GCC ≥ 14 makes implicit function declarations errors. Executing Step 6 died there: `ffs.c:1119: error: implicit declaration of function 'time'` (and `ctime` at :112/:536-537). Fixing those surfaced a second, same-class error — `lib/libutil/fparseln.c:101: error: assignment to 'char *' from 'int' ... [-Wint-conversion]` for the undeclared BSD function `fgetln`. **The cascade stops at exactly those two headers**, proven by building the fork directly: `CPPFLAGS="-include time.h -include bsd/stdio.h" gmake` produces `builddir/usr.sbin/makefs/makefs`, which runs, and whose symbols resolve correctly (`fgetln@LIBBSD_0.0`, `time`/`ctime` from glibc) — so the declarations are real fixes, not a `-Wno-error` papering-over that would have left truncated pointers on x86-64.
  - **`env.sh`**: add a pin `CHERI_MAKEFS_CPPFLAGS="-include time.h -include bsd/stdio.h"` (the repo doctrine: all pins live in env.sh).
  - **`scripts/cheri/build_image.sh`**: pass it as the environment of the **`makefs-linux` cheribuild invocation only** — e.g. export `CPPFLAGS="$CHERI_MAKEFS_CPPFLAGS"` immediately before `run_cheribuild makefs-linux` and unset it immediately after. It must not be in the environment for the `cheribsd-sysroot-*` / `disk-image-*` legs: those are FreeBSD cross-builds where an inherited `CPPFLAGS` would be picked up by `share/mk` and inject `bsd/stdio.h` into every translation unit, and that header does not exist in the FreeBSD sysroot. The fork's root GNUmakefile does `CPPFLAGS+=${EXTRA_CPPFLAGS}` and re-exports the combined value to its `bmake` sub-build, which is why an inherited value works at all.
  - The mechanism is verified, not guessed: the direct `gmake` build above used exactly this inherited-CPPFLAGS route.
- Re-run `python3 ./tmp/verification/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-scripts.py` — it gains two checks for this (the fix present, and scoped to the makefs leg). Turn it green.

**Superseded brief (staging rewrite round — landed in round 7):**
- **The change:** `scripts/cheri/stage_sources.sh` must stage each source tree as a **real git repository at the pinned commit**, not as a codeload tarball extract. cheribuild cannot accept a non-git tree: `Project.update()` calls `ensure_cloned()` *before* its `if skip_update: return` (`build/cheri/cheribuild-src/pycheribuild/projects/project.py:1354-1362`) and `GitRepository.ensure_cloned` clones whenever `(src_dir/".git")` is absent (`.../projects/repository.py:371-390`), so `--skip-update` cannot stop it and `--skip-clone` only makes it a fatal. Executing Step 5 proved it: `git clone … sources/qemu` → `destination path already exists and is not an empty directory`.
  - **Per tree** (qemu → `$SRC_ROOT/qemu`, cheribsd → `$SRC_ROOT/cheribsd`, makefs → `$SRC_ROOT/makefs-linux` — the last name stays as corrected): if the dest already holds a git repo whose `git rev-parse HEAD` equals the pin, reuse it; otherwise replace it and run `git init` + `git remote add origin <git-url>` + `git fetch --depth 1 origin <commit>` + `git checkout --detach FETCH_HEAD`, then **assert `git rev-parse HEAD` equals the pin** and die naming the tree otherwise. Verified working on the smallest repo before this brief was written: `build/cheri/scratch/gitprobe-1` (HEAD == the pin, `.git` present, shallow, correct content).
  - **`env.sh`**: the three `*_URL` pins become **git remotes** — `https://github.com/CHERI-Alliance/qemu`, `https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/cheribsd`, `https://github.com/Engil/makefs`. The three `*_COMMIT` pins do not change. Nothing else in the pin block moves.
  - **Keep**: the lane lock (`flock -n`), the 40-hex pin asserts, idempotence (a tree already at the pin is reused, never re-fetched; at most one `versions-stage.txt` line per pin), and the TOFU record — but its third field becomes the **staged commit's tree hash** (`git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}`) in place of the tarball sha256, which no longer exists.
  - **Gone**: the codeload route, `curl --max-time/--speed-limit`, the `.part` rename, and tarball sha256 recording. Removing the three already-staged tarball-extracted trees is part of the rewrite: they have no `.git`, so the reuse check will fall through to the staging path, which must **replace** the destination (`rm -rf "$dest"` on `build/cheri/sources/<name>` before `git init`). The stale `build/downloads/cheri/*.tar.gz` (~130 MB) can simply be left; nothing reads them.

Proven both ways: the current form gives `count=1 ["riscv64xcheri-softmmu morello-softmmu"]`; `IFS=, read -r -a _tgt_split <<< "$QEMU_TARGETS_CHERI"` gives `count=2`. **Fix it** — any correct split is acceptable (`IFS=,` on the pin directly is simplest; `IFS=' '` over the substituted form also works).
  2. **`build_image.sh` needs no change** — executing it reached its own `/usr/include/bsd/bsd.h` preflight (line 111) *after* passing the pin asserts, the lane lock, the staged-tree asserts **including the corrected `sources/makefs-linux`**, a real `cheribuild --list-targets` enumeration that found all five targets, and the libarchive/bsdtar/bmake checks. That stop is a genuine **host-layout** issue, not a script bug: Ubuntu's `libbsd-dev` ships the header at `/usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu/bsd/bsd.h`, while `makefs_linux.py` hard-codes `Path("/usr/include/bsd/bsd.h")` and fatals (`dependency_error(..., fatal=True)`) when it is absent. It is fixed on the host, not in the script — **do not "fix" the preflight**; it faithfully mirrors cheribuild's own check.

**Planner (Step 4 DONE 2026-10-15; Steps 5–6 BLOCKED on the package gate):**
- **Step 4 is complete except its package clause.** The fetch ran clean (`exit 0`, log `./tmp/logs/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-host-fetch.log`): `build/cheri/sources/{qemu,cheribsd,makefs-linux}` are staged — makefs **in the corrected dir name**, which is the defect this revision exists to fix — and `versions-stage.txt` carries all three pins with observed tarball sha256s: qemu `ffd47506927439916f913d26bab02c43ef5451fe84ff6c284b912a6df471f614`, cheribsd `1835a9fff8af8aa8f01cc852304c28f2afc3b93130586c01bdae91f44774e9ff`, makefs `445fdaeac3aee3359be0a36928f100988e64121198ab5db0925f3fbc92598763`. The three `env.sh` pins equal the Summary table, and the fetch did **not** modify `env.sh` (its 95/2 diff is round 1's pin block plus the two timeout lines).
- **The gate: all six Host Prerequisites are still MISSING** — discrete `dpkg-query` evidence in `./tmp/logs/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-host-prereqs.txt`. The fetch needs none of them, so it ran; **Steps 5 and 6 are blocked** until the user installs `sudo apt install -y libarchive-dev libarchive-tools libbsd-dev bmake libpixman-1-dev libslirp-dev`. Step 4's Evidence clause "the six packages verified present" is therefore **UNSATISFIED**: Step 4 is partially complete, and that failure detail must not be silently dropped at the next revision.
- **RESOLVED 2026-10-15: the staging design was rewritten to git-based staging (design A) and the coder round landed it.** Coder round 7 (10:23) rewrote `scripts/cheri/stage_sources.sh` (`git init` + `git remote add origin` + `git fetch --depth 1 origin "$commit"` + `git checkout --detach FETCH_HEAD` + a `rev-parse HEAD` assert, replacing the tarball route) and switched the three `*_URL` pins in `env.sh` to git remotes. Session report written (`build_result: pass`); the verifier is **58/58, exit 0**. **Hosted Step 4 is re-running now** via the hosting driver, log `./tmp/logs/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-host-fetch.log` — this is the rewrite's first execution and the real test of it; the previous tarball-route log is preserved beside it as `...-host-fetch-tarball-route.log`.
- **Historical — the blocker this resolved.** Step 5 attempt 4 (2026-10-15 09:25) had failed on a Plan Error in Step 1's staging design. Three attempts died on real defects first: (1) the `build_emulator.sh:64` IFS bug (fixed by the coder, verified); (2) `libcap-ng-dev` missing (installed); (3) the conda `python3` shadowing (worked around by the hosting driver `./tmp/verification/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-host-run.sh`, which prepends `/usr/bin` so cheribuild runs under `/usr/bin/python3`, which has `distutils`). Then the real one:
  **cheribuild refuses any source tree that is not a git repository.** Executing Step 5 gave `cd / && git clone --depth 1 --no-single-branch --branch main https://github.com/CHERI-Alliance/qemu.git …/sources/qemu` → `fatal: destination path … already exists and is not an empty directory`. Root cause, verified in the pin: `Project.update()` calls `self.ensure_cloned(...)` **before** its `if current_project.skip_update: return`, and `GitRepository.ensure_cloned` clones whenever `(src_dir / ".git")` is absent (`repository.py:371-390`); `--skip-clone` only converts that clone into a fatal `Sources for … missing!`. So **`--skip-update` can never prevent a clone** — a tarball-extracted tree can never satisfy cheribuild.
  **The plan's premise was wrong.** Its Summary claimed the "established pattern (from `build_toolchains.sh`)" was *codeload tarball → pre-stage → `--skip-update`*. That conflates two different things: `build_toolchains.sh` tarball-fetches **cheribuild itself** and only *asserts* that the LLVM source trees exist ("the planner stages it - this script never clones", `:88-89`). Those trees — staged by the planner on 2026-10-13, and **the only staging this repo has ever proven** — are real `git clone`s: `build/cheri/sources/llvm-project` has `.git` with `origin = github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/llvm-project.git`, and likewise `morello-llvm-project`.
  **Fix verified 2026-10-15 before proposing it** (scratch repo `build/cheri/scratch/gitprobe-1`): `git init` + `git remote add origin <url>` + `git fetch --depth 1 origin <commit>` + `git checkout --detach FETCH_HEAD` yields `rev-parse HEAD` == the pin, a real `.git`, shallow history, correct content. That is design (A) below. **The user must choose between (A) git-based staging**, **(B) fabricating a `.git` over the already hash-verified tarballs** (cheap, keeps today's TOFU hashes, but the repo metadata is not upstream's and QEMU's git-based version detection may object), or **(C) pausing the CHERI hosting**. Under (A) `env.sh`'s three `*_URL` pins become git URLs, the TOFU record's tarball-sha256 scheme becomes a commit/tree hash, and `stage_sources.sh` + the verifier's codeload checks are rewritten — a plan revision plus a coder round. **Nothing is retried until the user decides.**
- **Step 6 has a second, independent blocker that needs you (the user), not the coder:** all six apt packages are installed now, but `makefs-linux` additionally needs `/usr/include/bsd/bsd.h` to exist as a *path*, because `makefs_linux.py` tests exactly that path and fatals otherwise. Ubuntu's multiarch layout puts it elsewhere. One command fixes it, and it is a **host change — ask, never do it silently**: `sudo ln -s /usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu/bsd /usr/include/bsd`. Until then `build_image.sh` stops at its own line-111 preflight (verified by executing it) and cheribuild would fatal in `check_system_dependencies` even if our preflight were bypassed.
- Then: Step 5 (emulator, tens of minutes) → Step 6 (makefs-linux + both worlds/sysroots/disk images, multi-hour) as detached background jobs, then the tester round, then the Steps 7–8/10 coder round.
- **Note for the tester round's brief:** the IFS defect and the `makefs`-dir defect both slipped past structural verification because the scripts had never been *executed*. The tester's suite should add a behavioural check that each `scripts/cheri/*.sh` gets past its validation stage (e.g. by holding the lane lock so the script dies at `lane busy`, which is reached only after validation).
  <!-- UPDATED 2026-10-15: a THIRD instance, and the strongest one, because this time the structural checks went GREEN on a mechanism that did not work. The CWARNFLAGS.clang waiver passed all 67 checks and was ineffective (attempt 4). Two distinct blind spots are now demonstrated, and the tester's suite should cover both:
  (a) **Structure cannot validate a mechanism's EFFECT.** The verifier can only see that an option is named and passed; it cannot see that FreeBSD's build drops it before the compiler. Correspondingly, every hosted step's Evidence must be taken at the point of effect — for Step 6 that is the compile line / the produced artifact, never the invocation. This is the lesson the plan records in History for the waiver revision.
  (b) **A mechanism can be structurally present and functionally dead.** The waiver has since been REMOVED, and the verifier now asserts its absence in both files — a pin that does nothing reads as a working control, which is worse than no pin. The tester should assert the same class of thing behaviourally: no CHERI script may carry a knob that no code path consumes. -->

**Tester:**
- No tester round until the planner completes Steps 4–6; acceptance is Step 10.

| The **prompt-gated** re-send (round 36's fix) is still **not sufficient**: a prompt can belong to a **previously queued command's completion**, and the typed token echo can surface *after* the completion prompt. Observed live in run 3: cmd 6 (`cp`) was re-sent at **its own completion prompt** (the original `echo SPIKE_SYNC_6` surfaced later), then the **duplicate cp's prompt** triggered a re-send of cmd 7 (the runner) → **3 runner invocations**; a typed line can also carry line-editing control chars (`BENCH_D ^HIR`), so exact-text echo matching is fragile. Since the driver now sends only at a prompt with `--settle`, the dropped-typeahead case cannot occur — the re-send is pure risk. A **Plan Gap**: the 10–20 s re-send instruction (from the candidate-2 evidence) never contemplated prompt/token ordering. **User decision 2026-10-17: remove the re-send entirely (and the dead `--resend-timeout` knob); a missing token fails loudly at the sync deadline (raised to 2700 s — run 3 measured the corpus run at ≈1800 s).** Evidence: `./tmp/logs/2026-10-17-cheri-acceptance-run3-driver-reentrancy.txt` | Resolved | 2026-10-17 | 2026-10-17 | tester |

A missing token proves the typeahead was dropped **only when the guest is back at a prompt** — while a command runs it is expected. A **Plan Gap**: the re-send policy was designed for the login-drop race and never contemplated a legitimately long command. **User decision 2026-10-17: prompt-gated re-send + `--sync-timeout` default 900 s.** Evidence: `./tmp/logs/2026-10-17-cheri-acceptance-run2-findings.txt` §[1] | Resolved | 2026-10-17 | 2026-10-17 | tester |

## Guidance for Coder

**Files to modify:**

- `./env.sh`
- `./Makefile`
- `./scripts/common/build_corpus.sh`
- `./scripts/cheri/stage_sources.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/cheri/build_emulator.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/cheri/build_image.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/cheri/guest/runner` (new — the guest-side driver, baked or delivered per Step 7)
- `./scripts/cheri_riscv64/prepare.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/cheri_riscv64/run.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/morello/prepare.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/morello/run.sh` (new)
- `./scripts/common/derive_registry.py`

**Do NOT:** touch `benchmark/` (the submodule runs at its pinned commit; any needed bench change is a separate bench plan); touch `scripts/cheri/build_toolchains.sh`, `scripts/cheri/dialect_probe.sh` or `scripts/cheri/fix-text/` (historical record of the probe); rename any existing lane or flag; change `results/` layout conventions (stamp dirs, versions.txt, EXIT-trap collection — mirror `scripts/riscv64/run.sh`); **`chmod` any script** — every lane script is `-rw-rw-r--` by convention (`scripts/riscv64/*.sh`, `scripts/aarch64/*.sh`, and the three `scripts/cheri/*.sh`) and the Makefile invokes them as `bash scripts/…`, so an executable bit is neither needed nor wanted (the chmod attempt is what killed round 3).

**Lane-name vs bench-token mapping (applies to every step below):** `cheri_riscv64` and `aarch64-morello` are the **env lane names** (Makefile ARCH, results dirs, version_stamp lane argument). The **bench-facing tokens** are: `cheri_riscv64` (bench ARCH under `enable_riscv64_cheri=yes`) and **`aarch64`** (bench Makefile:382-383 forces `ARCH := aarch64` under `enable_aarch64_morello=yes` — the audit corrected the line citation from a stale `:374-375`). Skip lists and `CSB_TARGET_ARCH` use the bench token; `--lane` for derive_registry uses the bench token too; free-text fields (skip-reason) carry the human lane name.

**Step-by-step with verification:** steps carry `(auto)` checks (mechanical commands whose output is the check) and `(scripted)` checks (run the named script under `./tmp/verification/`; exit 0 = pass). Steps 1–3 are coder-owned **scripts whose checks are structural/dry-run only** — do not attempt to run the fetches or builds to completion in a round; the planner hosts their execution as Steps 4–6 (round wall clock is 3600 s; the builds run for hours). Steps 7 and 8 are coder-owned, but **Step 8 belongs to the round after the planner's hosting**; Step 9 is planner-owned; Step 10 is coder+tester with the classification decision surfaced to the planner.

- **Temporary:** `./tmp/verification/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-lanes.sh` — end-to-end lane acceptance per lane (cheri_riscv64, aarch64-morello). If artifacts are missing the script **stops and reports which hosted step must run** — it must not itself invoke the multi-hour `build_emulator.sh`/`build_image.sh` inside a tester round (the round is capped at 3600 s and the builds are planner-hosted by design; a tester round that starts one dies mid-build). **UPDATED 2026-10-17 (run 6, after the Phase 6 fix round 39): verify the FINAL stamps — `results/cheri_riscv64/20261017T132840Z/` and `results/aarch64-morello/20261017T135359Z/`** (run 5's `20261017T085733Z` / `20261017T092154Z` and run 4's `20261017T073711Z` / `20261017T080425Z` remain prior acceptance evidence; run 6 reproduced run 5's verdict sets exactly) **— the script VERIFIES them rather than re-running the lanes** (a full two-lane deploy+run is ≈50–60 min, i.e. the whole tester window; the full re-run is planner-hosted). Assert for each stamp: `results.json` carries the full derived record set (246), skipped records as 1025 (**exactly 4 on cheri_riscv64, exactly 3 on aarch64-morello** after the skip table), `guest-status.txt` says `run-test-exit: 0`, `versions.txt` names the emulator binary and the guest image (lines appended by run.sh), and the corpus manifest's entries match the classification table (**141 units, zero non-built on cheri_riscv64; 142 and zero on aarch64-morello** — the skipped programs have no units entry). Then exercise the **124-no-publish** path cheaply through the overridable pin: `make ARCH=<lane> run LANE_RUN_TIMEOUT=5` (the driver caps at 5 s, exits 124, and **no** stamp is published) — `env.sh` honours a pre-set value (`: "${LANE_RUN_TIMEOUT:=3600}"`) and the Makefile forwards it; also assert the two defaults did not move (3600 / 7200) and `git -C benchmark status --short` is empty after the exercise.
- **Temporary:** `./tmp/verification/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-classify.sh` — reads each lane's corpus manifest (the CHERI lanes' manifests are `build/<env-lane>/payload/manifest.json`, e.g. `build/cheri_riscv64/payload/manifest.json` and `build/aarch64-morello/payload/manifest.json`) plus `results.json` against `configure.json` and produces the skip-classification table: (a) corpus-build failures from the manifest's build-failed entries (never `1025`+`make-status` — the in-guest `no-make` runs produce no make attempts, so `make-status` cannot appear; `scheduler/run-test.cpp:715-721` sets it only on a failed make attempt); (b) recognized bench-side extraction gaps as their own pre-labelled bucket (e.g. `script/get_x64_func_inst.sh` has no cheri_riscv64 branch; `acc-read-func`'s extraction can fail for format reasons, not CHERI semantics); (c) verdicts outside `expect-results` (candidate CHERI semantics); (d) leftovers (genuine defects). Feeds Step 10. **UPDATED 2026-10-17 (the original classification ran on run 4's stamps; this is the expectation on run 6's stamps `results/cheri_riscv64/20261017T132840Z/` and `results/aarch64-morello/20261017T135359Z/`; run 5's table `...-classification-run5.json` stays as the baseline):** bucket (a) EMPTY (the three skip candidates are no longer staged — their programs have no manifest units entry), `1025` counts **4** on `cheri_riscv64` and **3** on `aarch64-morello` (the two asmfunc records plus each lane's skip rows), bucket (b) unchanged (2), bucket (c) unchanged (25), bucket (d) empty, **no unexplained entries**. Any bucket-(c) record appearing as `1025` is a regression — those are bench-side semantics decisions and must stay real verdicts.
- **Temporary migrations:** `./tmp/verification/2026-10-12-test-env-all-archs-step1.py` (its CHERI section asserts the lanes fail with "not provisioned" — rewrite it to the dry-run form used in Step 8's checks: `make -n ARCH=cheri_riscv64 deploy` prints the script invocations, no "not provisioned"; drop the must-fail assertions) and `...-step2.py` (re-run after the Step 10 `derive_registry.py` edit as the regression check that the riscv64 derived output stays byte-identical).
- **Permanent:** none — the lane runs are the persistent acceptance, repeated by every future lane re-run.

- **Temporary:** `./tmp/verification/2026-10-14-cheri-lanes-bringup-bringup-mode.sh` — the corpus builder's tolerant mode: stage a scratch corpus with one forced unit failure whose derived side file is therefore absent, run the builder in `--bring-up` mode, and assert it finalizes the manifest and exits 0 with the missing side file recorded as a non-built entry; then run strict mode and assert it still exits non-zero on the same input. The plan's predicted CHERI failure class is exactly this case (`acc-read-func`'s opcode extraction), so tolerating only the unit loop would leave the mode useless.

**Tests to investigate for retirement:** No test obsolescence identified — the probe (`dialect_probe.sh`) and its vendored fix-texts remain as historical record; their removal, if ever, is a separate cleanup.

**Boundary to state in the tester report:** both CHERI lanes consume a **derived registry** (like riscv64) and run the prebuilt corpus in-guest with `no-make`. On smoke runs (before the bench-side indepassembly translation lands) the two asmfunc records are `1025`-skipped with a skip-reason (they are in the bring-up skip table), or build-failed in the manifest when outside the table; either way they are expected, not lane defects. No-workaround acceptance re-run follows the successor sweep.

## Summary

**Problem.** The bench's two CHERI ports exist but no CHERI emulator, guest image or lane script does: `make ARCH=cheri_riscv64 …` and `make ARCH=aarch64-morello …` exit non-zero with a "not provisioned" message (Makefile:202-244), and only the assembler-level dialect probe is implemented. The deferral `cheri-lanes-bringup-deferred` records the full scope: emulators (CHERI-Alliance/CTSRD QEMU), CheriBSD purecap images, lane scripts, and end-to-end `exhausted-run` acceptance. Probe verdicts in hand (results/cheri/probe-2026-10-13.json): cheri_riscv64 dialect **accepted** (the vendored fix text assembles); **morello rejected under purecap** — its bodies are "pending" only in the sense that the translation lands in the bench plan `2026-10-14-indepassembly-return-site-translation` (B1) Step 4, whose Step 4e fallback can instead fire a fresh deferral (see Step 10's contingency).

**What is already known (verified against the pinned cheribuild at `build/cheri/cheribuild-src`, pin `db2f036`):**

- **One emulator build serves both lanes.** cheribuild target `qemu` (`pycheribuild/projects/build_qemu.py:583`, repo `https://github.com/CHERI-Alliance/qemu.git` branch `main`) builds `riscv64xcheri-softmmu` and `morello-softmmu` among its default targets → binaries `qemu-system-riscv64xcheri` and `qemu-system-morello` (`build_qemu.py:616,625`). `qemu_bindir = cheri_sdk_bindir` (`chericonfig.py:815-816`), so with the existing `--output-root build/cheri/output` layout flags the emulators land in `build/cheri/output/sdk/bin/` next to the CHERI-LLVM we already built.
- **Guest images are full-userland disk images** (Q3). The pin contains both families — the MFS-root/minimal machinery (`BuildCheriBsdMfsKernel` at `cross/cheribsd.py:2114`, `BuildCheriBsdMfsImageAndKernels` at :2223, `disk_image.BuildMfsRootCheriBSDDiskImage` at `disk_image.py:1430`, all subclassing the *minimal* image class whose root comes from `pycheribuild/files/minimal-image/*.files`, cheribsdbox-based) and the full one (`disk_image.BuildCheriBSDDiskImage` at `disk_image.py:1444`, prefix `cheribsd`, assembling a root from the built world). **Q3 selected the full one**: `disk-image-<xtarget>` = `BuildCheriBSDDiskImage`, so the guest root is a complete userland, not the cheribsdbox set. The MFS/minimal classes are recorded here as *not used* — an earlier draft of this plan described them as the lane images, which the audit flagged as contradictory.
- **The content-injection lever for our chosen target is the `extra-files` directory**, `disk_image.py:171-180` (`BuildDiskImageBase`, default `$SOURCE_ROOT/extra-files`) — not `extra-files-lists`, which is declared only on `BuildMinimalCheriBSDDiskImage` (`disk_image.py:1109-1112`) and consumed only there (`:1164`). Since Q3 chose the full image class, `extra-files` is the lever that applies; `extra-files-lists` is noted only as the minimal-class equivalent.
- **The bench-side corpus vehicle is system-mode only.** `scripts/common/build_corpus.sh` currently accepts only lane `riscv64` (:25-32, "only the riscv64 corpus is provisioned"), hard-wires `RISCV64_CXX/ASM/OBJDUMP` + the riscv64 shim (:52-60), and aborts (exit 1) when any unit fails outside `expected_failures` (:149-152). The aarch64 lane is qemu-user (host scheduler, units built on demand — no corpus staging); the CHERI lanes are system-mode like riscv64 and must extend this builder.
- The toolchains needed by the bench build are already present: `build/cheri/output/{sdk,morello-sdk}` (cheri_riscv64: `sdk/bin/clang++ --target=riscv64-unknown-freebsd` with `-march=rv64gcxcheri -mabi=l64pc128d`; morello: `morello-sdk/bin/clang++ --target=aarch64-unknown-freebsd` with `-march=morello -mabi=purecap` — mirror `scripts/cheri/dialect_probe.sh`'s recorded invocations).
- Host support for image building on Linux is present in the pin (`projects/makefs_linux.py`, `bmake.py`, `bsdtar.py`) — but only **`bsdtar`** is load-bearing for this plan: the disk-image leg uses the FreeBSD host-tools makefs cheribuild builds itself, and the `makefs_linux.py`/`bmake.py` targets are the fork this plan **removed** on 2026-10-15 (see Host Prerequisites).
- **The staging pattern is GIT-BASED, not tarball-based — corrected 2026-10-15 after executing Step 5.** The earlier text here claimed the env's established pattern was "pin → codeload tarball → pre-stage → `--skip-update`", citing `build_toolchains.sh`. That was a **misreading**: that script tarball-fetches **cheribuild itself** and merely *asserts* the LLVM source trees exist ("the planner stages it - this script never clones", `build_toolchains.sh:88-89`) — and those trees, staged by the planner on 2026-10-13 and the only staging this repo has ever proven, are real **`git clone`s** (`build/cheri/sources/llvm-project/.git` → `origin = github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/llvm-project.git`). The distinction is load-bearing because **cheribuild cannot accept a non-git source tree at all**: `Project.update()` calls `ensure_cloned()` *before* its `if current_project.skip_update: return` (`project.py:1354-1362`), and `GitRepository.ensure_cloned` clones whenever `(src_dir / ".git")` is absent (`repository.py:371-390`); `--skip-clone` merely converts that clone into a fatal `Sources for … missing!`. Executing Step 5 produced exactly it: `git clone --depth 1 … https://github.com/CHERI-Alliance/qemu.git …/sources/qemu` → `fatal: destination path … already exists and is not an empty directory`. **User decision 2026-10-15: design (A) — stage the trees as real git repositories** via `git init` + `git remote add origin <git-url>` + `git fetch --depth 1 origin <commit>` + `git checkout --detach FETCH_HEAD`, which was verified on a scratch repo (`build/cheri/scratch/gitprobe-1`: HEAD == the pin, `.git` present, shallow, correct content) before adoption. The three `*_URL` pins therefore hold **git remotes**, and the TOFU record's tarball-sha256 becomes a commit/tree hash. Note `version_stamp.sh` **truncates** its output file (:46) — build-side scripts must NOT re-invoke it on a shared file (see Step 1's versions protocol).

**Hosting split (user decision 2026-10-14, Q1).** Steps 1–3 stay coder-written scripts — `make deploy` must work on a cold clone — but their **execution** is planner-hosted as Steps 4–6, because a round's wall clock is capped at `OPERATE_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 3600 s` (`~/bin/claude_utils.py:401`) and these builds run for hours. The planner writes its hosting drivers under `./tmp/verification/` (the only tree it may write) and runs them as monitored background jobs; the scripts themselves remain the reproducible source of truth, verified idempotently in the coder round against the artifacts the hosted run produced. **This means the planner authors files under `build/`** (staged source trees, cheribuild output) — that is the user-directed hosting role, the same pattern that staged the two LLVM trees on 2026-10-13, and it is not a plan step the coder owns.

**Pinned inputs the hosting steps stage** (commits resolved 2026-10-14 against the live repos; the three codeload tarball routes were verified HTTP 200 that day, but the staging route is now **git** — a tarball cannot satisfy cheribuild, see the staging-pattern bullet):

| Input | Pin | Staged at |
|---|---|---|
| QEMU (`CHERI-Alliance/qemu`, branch `main`) | `32bf55e2965dcdcfb0139d209e7a7b9dd1314583` (2026-10-13) | `build/cheri/sources/qemu` |
| CheriBSD (`CTSRD-CHERI/cheribsd`, branch `main`) | `88f39900c32928d807dba245fba138808c666f34` (2026-07-20) | `build/cheri/sources/cheribsd` |
| **riscv-pk — the riscv64 BBL boot firmware** (`CTSRD-CHERI/riscv-pk`, branch `cheri_purecap`) | **`0cb596cf6e49c0014256300b614c5cd30cb90d57`** (resolved live 2026-10-16) | **`build/cheri/sources/bbl`** — cheribuild's own dir name, not `riscv-pk` |
| ~~`makefs` (`Engil/makefs` default branch)~~ | ~~`8aad78f7f7d7a503aa415f3700099afe35a136e6` (2021-10-15)~~ | **REMOVED 2026-10-15** (Plan Error; see Host Prerequisites) |

<!-- UPDATED 2026-10-15: the makefs row is RETIRED — the fork cannot build the disk's msdos EFI partition (`Unknown fs type 'msdos'`), so it is no longer staged, pinned or invoked (user decision). Struck through rather than deleted so the pin that broke attempt 5 stays auditable. -->
**All three trees are staged as real git repositories at those commits** (design A, user decision 2026-10-15) — see the staging-pattern bullet below; the `*_URL` pins therefore hold **git remotes** (`https://github.com/…`), not codeload tarball routes.

The source-directory names are cheribuild's own: `BuildQEMU` leaves `default_directory_basename` unset so it resolves to the target name `qemu` (`simple_project.py:1935-1936`), and `BuildCHERIBSD` sets `"cheribsd"` explicitly (`cross/cheribsd.py:1868`) — both verified 2026-10-15 by importing the pin's classes and reading `default_directory_basename` (the same check produced `makefs-linux`, the third name retired with its tree; see Host Prerequisites). One cheribsd tree serves both xtargets — `per_target_branches` overrides only the rvy/zcheri093 targets (`cross/cheribsd.py:1870-1876`), not ours.

**Design decisions:**

- **Boot vehicle — per-lane argv, both booting the pinned disk as rootfs** (user decision 2026-10-15, after the audit found the riscv64 disk-only boot unreachable). `QemuOptions.can_boot_kernel_directly` (`qemu_utils.py:49`) decides each lane's path, and `qemu_utils.py:215-219` uses a kernel file **only** when it is true:

  | Lane | Emulator argv (beyond the pins) | Rootfs |
  |---|---|---|
  | `cheri_riscv64` | `-M virt -bios "$QEMU_BIOS_CHERI_RISCV64" -kernel "$QEMU_KERNEL_CHERI_RISCV64" -drive file=<pinned disk>,format=raw,if=virtio` | the pinned `disk-image-riscv64-purecap` |
  | `aarch64-morello` | `-M virt,gic-version=3 -cpu morello -bios "$QEMU_BIOS_CHERI_MORELLO" -drive file=<pinned disk>,format=raw,if=virtio` | the pinned `disk-image-morello-purecap` |

  Both lanes boot the same *kind* of artifact (a full-userland disk image, per Q3), but the **kernel line necessarily differs**: Morello purecap sets `can_boot_kernel_directly = False` (`qemu_utils.py:54`, `# boot from disk`) and reaches its kernel through UEFI → `loader(8)`, while riscv64 sets it True (`:80`) and must be handed a kernel entry — in the pinned QEMU `hw/riscv/virt.c` leaves `kernel_entry = 0` when neither `-kernel` nor a pflash unit is given, and the pin's `pc-bios/` ships no RISC-V S-mode firmware (only `opensbi-riscv{32,64}-generic-fw_dynamic.bin`), so a disk-only riscv64 boot has no transition that can succeed. This is cheribuild's own riscv64 CHERI flow (`qemu_utils.py:215-219` emits `-kernel`).
  **The riscv64 row also needs its firmware — corrected 2026-10-16 after round 1's boot failed.** `-kernel` is mandatory but **not sufficient**: the CHERI riscv64 machine loads the BBL firmware named by `RISCV64_BIOS_BIN` (`sources/qemu/include/hw/riscv/boot.h:30`) into M-mode and jumps to the payload at the kernel load address from there. cheribuild's own flow passes both (`run_qemu.py:245-262` → `-bios <bbl>`, `qemu_utils.py:215-219` → `-kernel <kernel>`), and depends on the `bbl-baremetal-riscv64-purecap` target (`run_qemu.py:938`). So the asymmetry is **firmware + kernel for riscv64, firmware only (no `-kernel`) for Morello** — a future reader must not "simplify" the riscv64 row to either half alone.
  **And the riscv64 CPU pin is not empty — corrected 2026-10-16 after round 26's clock-init panic.** The lane pins `QEMU_CPU_CHERI_RISCV64="rv64,sstc=off"`: QEMU advertises Sstc but gates the S-mode `stimecmp` CSR on `menvcfg.STCE`, which the pinned BBL never sets, so the kernel must be pushed onto its own `sbi_set_timer` fallback (implemented by BBL, `mtrap.c:92,171`) instead of trapping. The spelling is the machine's **own** default model (`virt`'s `default_cpu_type` *is* the concrete `rv64`), so it is one property, not a CPU swap — CHERI lives in the xcheri binary, and the A/B diagnostic proved a purecap kernel boots under it. Cost stays low: the *world* build is required for both lanes anyway (it produces the purecap sysroot the corpus cross-compile links against), and assembling a disk image on top of a built world is minutes, not hours. The `edk2-aarch64-code.fd` firmware needs no apt package — the pinned QEMU ships it (`pc-bios/edk2-aarch64-code.fd.bz2`, verified in the pin's `pc-bios/` listing); `qemu-efi-aarch64` (apt candidate `2025.11-3ubuntu7.3`, **not** installed) is the named fallback if QEMU's datadir lookup fails.
- **Guest network containment is pinned, not prose** — the riscv64 lane's `run.sh` passes no `-net` flags and QEMU then creates a default user-mode (slirp) netdev with `restrict=off`, so a guest running benchmark attack code can route IP out through the host. Both CHERI lanes therefore pin their netdev in `env.sh`: `-net none` for any non-network winner (9p / baked-corpus / result-disk), and `-netdev user,restrict=on,...` alongside the loopback `hostfwd` for the SSH winner (`restrict=on` isolates the guest from host and internet while leaving explicitly-set forwarding rules working).
- **Host prerequisites are a gate, not an afterthought** — the audit found the pinned cheribuild hard-requires packages this host does not have (verified MISSING: `pkg-config libarchive`, `bsdtar`, `libpixman-1-dev`, `libslirp-dev`, and — found by executing Step 5 — `libcap-ng-dev`), and `BuildFreeBSDBase.check_system_dependencies` (`cheribsd.py:630-636`) fatals on a non-FreeBSD host without `libarchive-dev` **before any world build starts**. The full set and the reason for each are in **Host prerequisites** below; installing them is a user action. **The `makefs` question is settled: cheribuild builds its own FreeBSD host-tools makefs and this plan uses that** — the `Engil/makefs` fork this plan once pinned was a Plan Error (no msdos support; frozen in 2021) and is removed entirely (see Host prerequisites).
- **Emulator target list narrowed** (user decision Q2) — build `qemu` with `--qemu/targets=riscv64xcheri-softmmu,morello-softmmu`. These are the **final** configure names: the pin's `riscv64cheri-softmmu` → `riscv64xcheri-softmmu` rewrite (`build_qemu.py:426-427`) is gated on `self._get_config_option_handle("qemu_targets").is_default_value` (line 418), so setting the option bypasses the rewrite and the spelled names must already be final.
- **Guest I/O** — the spike (Step 7) selects among three first-class candidates: (1) virtio-9p share (in+out; `security_model=mapped-xattr`, corpus-in mounted read-only) — **no kernel work is needed for (1)**: `p9fs.ko` and `virtio_p9fs.ko` are already built into and installed in **both** pinned images (planner-verified *inside the images*, 2026-10-16: directory entries and the `p9fs_mount` symbol), so the guest only has to `kldload` them, or let `devmatch` auto-load the virtio driver via its PNP info; the "p9fs kernel-config gap" reported after round 1 was a **withdrawn finding** (Issue Log) and no rebuild is authorized; (2) SSH over qemu user-net bound to loopback (`hostfwd=tcp:127.0.0.1:PORT-:22`) with cheribuild's generated test key (`build/cheri/build/insecure_test_ssh_key{,.pub}`, generated on first use — `chericonfig.py:819-824`) and its public half staged into the image as `authorized_keys` via the extra-files hook; (3) the image's `extra-files` directory for corpus-in, paired with a second out-channel (a writable 9p share or a result disk) since a baked-in corpus cannot return results. Candidate (2) is better supported than it first appears: the disk-image machinery generates sshd **host** keys on its own (`disk_image.py:1052-1075`, `generate_ssh_host_keys`). Criterion: **two-way capability** (in AND out) — a one-way winner pairs with a second channel. A per-lane winner is allowed. Console parsing is avoided (the riscv64 lane's doctrine).

  **Candidate (3) may win only where the payload stays replaceable per run.** Its "in" side is baked at image-build time, but Step 10's confirmatory loop re-derives the registry (injected skips) and re-runs — with an image-baked corpus and a baked `configure.json`, the injected skips can never reach the guest and the loop's "skipped ones as 1025 / no unexplained entries" states are unreachable. So candidate (3) is admissible **only** paired with a per-run-writable channel for the derived corpus + registry (the corpus-in may be baked; the registry and payload that the loop rewrites may not), and the step records which parts are baked. Otherwise the spike must pick (1) or (2). Any mechanism that changes image content also carries the rebuild obligation recorded in the image/pin feedback loop below.

- **Image/pin feedback loop (must be explicit — the audit found this transition missing).** Step 6 builds the images and fills the `env.sh` sha256 pins; Step 7 may then discover that a mechanism needs content *inside* the image (SSH `authorized_keys`, a baked runner, a baked corpus). The sequence is therefore: (i) **reserve what can be reserved before the build** — the audit's security finding makes one such reservation mandatory regardless of the spike (see Host prerequisites / Step 3: pre-seed `extra-files/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` with the test key so the build cannot self-answer "yes" to baking the developer's personal `~/.ssh/*.pub`); (ii) if the winning mechanism needs more baked content, the image is **rebuilt** by re-running `build_image.sh` (planner-hosted; on top of the already-built world this is minutes) with the staged `extra-files` content, which re-fills the pins; (iii) the deploy recipe's image leg is **hash-aware, not existence-aware**, so a stale pinned image is rebuilt rather than silently used; (iv) Step 6's evidence notes that the pin is re-taken whenever the image is rebuilt. Whoever authors the staged extra-files content is the spike's owner (coder), and leftovers are listed and removed before the final rebuild.
  **It fired for real on 2026-10-16.** Adding the bbl target to `build_image.sh` made the firmware install into `output/sdk/`, which cheribuild reads as "the SDK changed": it re-installed **both** worlds (install-only), repackaged both sysroots and **reassembled both disk images**. Both sha256 pins were therefore re-taken (`afdcb05d…e9121`, `00437fdd…a9215`) and the new bytes were re-verified at the point of effect (both still carry `p9fs.ko`/`virtio_p9fs.ko`/`loader.efi`). **Nothing proven against the old images carries forward** — Morello's boot is re-run in round 26 rather than cited.
- **Workarounds allowed at bring-up** — the first full run uses `disable_independent_assembly=yes` and a per-lane skip table; the no-workaround acceptance re-run is the successor env follow-up's sweep (after B1 lands) — per the user's "skip test cases if you need".
- **Parallel-safe with B1.** This plan touches only the env repo. Shared files with B1's successor follow-up: `env.sh` (this plan appends a new pin block; the follow-up edits only the `BENCH_COMMIT` line), `scripts/common/build_corpus.sh` (this plan parameterizes the lane/toolchain; the follow-up removes `disable_independent_assembly` from its invocation), and `scripts/common/derive_registry.py` (this plan adds a per-(lane,record) table; the follow-up deletes the riscv64/aarch64 asmfunc rows — disjoint entries by design). The lane lock serializes all lane/build work; lane runs must not start while the bench submodule is dirty (Step 10's prerequisite).
- **Cost profile** (all resumable, run in background like `build_toolchains.sh`): the `qemu` build narrowed to its two targets (user decision Q2) is the shortest of the three; each CheriBSD **world** build is a multi-hour cross-build and is the long pole, with disk-image assembly adding little on top of it. The `cheribsd-sysroot-<xtarget>` and `disk-image-<xtarget>` targets are both built per lane, and both depend on the world build, so the world is compiled once per lane. Host: 12 cores, 75 GB RAM, 3.5 TB free (`build/cheri` currently 18 GB). A build failure is a planner blocker — report the failing step, never retry blindly.
- **Timeout layering (the audit found the nested cap makes a cold-clone `make deploy` impossible).** The shared `LANE_DEPLOY_TIMEOUT=7200` cannot wrap a multi-hour world build — the outer 2 h cap would fire mid-build (exit 124) before `CHERI_BUILD_TIMEOUT` ever matters. So the CHERI `deploy` recipe wraps **only** the cheap legs (sources-if-missing, prepare) in `LANE_DEPLOY_TIMEOUT`, and wraps the emulator and image script invocations in the CHERI-specific `CHERI_BUILD_TIMEOUT`; the shared pin is left untouched for the other lanes. `make -n` is expected to show the intended timeout per line. `CHERI_BUILD_TIMEOUT` is the single bound on CHERI build work; the planner-hosted Steps 5–6 run the scripts directly, outside `make`, so the hosting path and the cold-clone path share one bound.
- **Timeout pins become overridable for the 124 exercise.** `env.sh` currently assigns `LANE_RUN_TIMEOUT`/`LANE_DEPLOY_TIMEOUT` unconditionally, and both `run.sh` models re-source `env.sh`, so no environment or `make` override can reach the timeout — the tester's prescribed 124-no-publish exercise cannot take effect as written (the audit reproduced this on the exact model). This plan changes those two assignments to honor a pre-set value (`: "${LANE_RUN_TIMEOUT:=3600}"`) and has the CHERI `run` recipe forward `LANE_RUN_TIMEOUT=$(LANE_RUN_TIMEOUT)`. **Defaults are unchanged**, so no lane's behavior moves; this is a documented semantic change to `env.sh`'s assignment style, not a change to any pin's value.

**Alternatives considered:** building CHERI QEMU outside cheribuild — rejected (cheribuild already pins the repo/branch and installs into the existing layout); downloading prebuilt CheriBSD release images — no first-class download target found in this pin; keeping the lanes scaffolded — rejected (that is exactly the deferral).

**Out of scope:** any `benchmark/` change; the probe/fix-text cleanup; the post-B1 no-workaround acceptance (successor follow-up).
